186:## Gold standard: the Contrapositive lesson page (approved 2026-10-12)
187-
188-Joshua called the finished page "perfect". Treat it as the template for every other concept lecture: same order, same density, same rules. Do not copy its wording or its examples. Rework the structure for the new topic and pick fresh examples.
189-
190-**Where it lives:** `ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-bootcamp/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/` (`OpeningCourseContent.tsx` `ContrapositiveNote` and `FourRules`, `ContrapositiveVisuals.tsx`, `contrapositive-visuals.css`), commit `383747e9` plus the uncommitted follow-ups of 2026-10-12. Video project: `ai-os-projects/contrapositive-video/lecture` (`script.json`, `index.html`, `build_audio.py`). Full correction log: `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md`.
191-
192-### Page shape (about 600 words of text, seven sections)
193-
194-1. **What it is.** Plain definition first, in a navy panel, then one short example. No run-up.
195-2. **Why it matters or what it gives you.** Two or three sentences. The key term pulses, one exclamation at most.
196-3. **Watch the lesson.** A 60 to 90 second narrated video placed after the idea is introduced. Captions track, poster image, written fallback that only renders when opened or when the video fails.
197-4. **How to write it.** Two bold steps (Step 1 and Step 2), then an interactive demo with "Step 1. Click here to ..." and "Step 2. Click here to ..." buttons. A second demo uses a fresh, simple, intuitive example where one side already starts negated, so the rule that two nots cancel is shown, not stated. Each demo shows the rule as an if-then sentence above the diagram and the result as an if-then sentence below.
198-5. **What it is not.** One line, then slim cards: red "Wrong" for each look-alike, green "Right" for the answer. Badge, one-line change, diagram. Nothing else.
199-6. **A short habit paragraph.** One paragraph, no list.
200-7. **Pop Quiz.** Centered title with a letter-pop and light-sweep effect. Four items, one at a time. Ideas shown as short phrases, never letters. Right answer explanation is one sentence (the answer as a natural if-then). Wrong answer explanation is "Not that one." plus one specific reason about that choice. No intro paragraph, no category tag lines.
201-
202-### What Joshua wanted (keep)
203-
204-- One main example carried through the page, plus one fresh second example. Never the same example in more places than that, and never an example already used on another page.
205-- Natural if-then sentences for every rule and result. Diagrams support the sentence; they never replace it.
206-- Bold numbered steps. Controls say what to click. The student drives; nothing autoplays.
207-- Colour carries meaning and travels with the idea (amber sufficient, teal necessary). Wrong looks wrong (red, X); right looks right (green, check).
208-- Sleek, high-end visuals. A new effect for each new moment; never reuse the same trick. Every animation has a still equivalent and stops under reduced motion.
209-- Short. When a page feels like too much, cut sections, not words: remove whole parts that repeat what the video, a demo or the quiz already does.
210-- Ideas in quiz diagrams are condensed statements ("On the list", "Wristband"), not letter codes.
211-- Narrated video in his voice standard, AI voice acceptable, built from the same wording as the page.
212-
213-### What Joshua rejected (never repeat)
214-
215-- A squiggle (~) for negation anywhere. Use the word "not" or a diagonal strike from the idea's top left to bottom right.
216-- The raffle, coat, coffee, breathing, elevator, door and any example already used elsewhere. "Door", "patterns" and "move" as student words.
217-- Sophistry and obligation metaphors ("promise", "depends on", "nothing to stand on"). Stock encouragement and run-ups ("Here's the good news", "Every time:").
218-- Content repeated on the page, a legend that restates what a pill already says, a paragraph before the quiz explaining the quiz, tag lines that add nothing.
219-- Complicated interactives: the first light-bulb stage (wires, chips, extra buttons) and the "four worlds" picker were both cut as too busy. One idea, one control, one sentence.
220-- Long answer explanations. Explanations that talk about the diagram ("both ideas get a not") instead of saying the answer in plain English.
221-- Letters standing in for ideas.
222-
223-### Process that produced it (work backwards from this)
224-
225-1. Write the section list above for the new topic before any copy. Cut anything the video, a demo or the quiz already covers.
226-2. Draft prose in Joshua's voice, then run the `humanizer` skill over it and the corrections log. No em dashes.
227-3. Build the visuals one at a time, check each on the live preview (not only in tests), and ask what is new about each effect.
228-4. Pin the page order and the key phrases in the content test; update the test when a section is deliberately cut.
229-5. Video: one script file, narration synthesized per sentence, word timings for sync, scenes built in the page's own palette, rendered and checked frame by frame. Voice: Kokoro af_heart (author grade A) when no paid voice key exists.
230-6. Log every new correction in `wiki/COPY-CORRECTIONS.md` and here before moving on.
231-
232-### Necessary definition and careful door metaphor, 2026-10-13
233-Explicit direction: Joshua's wording "The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens." leads the "Where the certainty is" section of the Necessary page. The door metaphor from his source may be used carefully (sufficient opens the door; necessary is guaranteed on the other side). This narrows the earlier blanket rejection of "door" as a student word; "door" as a unit of content stays banned. Avoid copy that sounds AI-written. Acceptance: read the section aloud; the metaphor must not suggest cause, timing, or that the necessary condition is only true behind the door.

231	      <div className="cr-sufficiency">
232	        <p className="cr-sufficiency-lead"><strong>A sufficient condition is the “if” part of a rule. When it is true, the “then” part has to be true too.</strong></p>
233	        <p>For example, in the rule below, being enrolled in law school is the <strong>sufficient condition</strong>. Being a student is the “then” part. It's what the rule guarantees.</p>
234	        <blockquote><strong>If</strong> you're enrolled in law school, <strong>then</strong> you're a student.</blockquote>
235	        <p>Someone might be enrolled. Someone might not. The rule doesn't say which. It only says what follows if they are.</p>
236	        <p>Nothing else has to be true for the guarantee to hold. If someone is enrolled in law school, they have to be a student.</p>
237	        <p>If they aren't enrolled, the rule says nothing. They might be a student or they might not.</p>

266	      <details className="cr-extension"><summary>Two more Group 1 indicators</summary><p><strong>Where</strong> can state a rule across locations: “Where a collection point operates, parcels can be returned.” An operating collection point proves the availability of returns there. Availability is not proof that someone made a return.</p><p><strong>The only</strong> needs its complete phrase: “The only notebooks on this shelf are hardback.” A notebook on the shelf is hardback. The rule does not put every hardback notebook there.</p><p>These are part of your Group 1 work. Understand what the full phrase covers before using its group as a placement shortcut.</p></details>
267	      <p>We have seen what can be enough. Now let's name what that condition requires.</p>
268	    </ReferenceStep>
269	
270	    <ReferenceStep number={4} label="The necessary condition" title="Necessary means required">
271	      <div className="cr-sufficiency nc-lesson">
272	        <p className="cr-sufficiency-lead"><strong>A necessary condition is a requirement. It has to be met, but meeting it might not be enough.</strong></p>
273	        <p>For example, you have to be a student to be enrolled in law school. That makes being a student <strong>necessary for law-school enrollment</strong>.</p>
274	        <NecessaryConditionStatement/>
275	        <p>But being a student doesn’t mean you’re enrolled in law school. You could be studying history instead.</p>
276	        <p className="nc-takeaway"><strong>Being a student is required. It doesn’t prove enrollment on its own.</strong></p>
277	        <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation nc-certainty" aria-label="Where the certainty is">
278	          <h3>Where the certainty is</h3>
279	          <p><strong>The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens.</strong></p>
280	          <p>Look at the name. It's called necessary because it is necessary. If someone is enrolled in law school, being a student has to be true.</p>
281	          <p>The sufficient condition opens the door. Once it's open, the necessary condition is guaranteed to be on the other side. That makes it <PulseWord>the place of certainty.</PulseWord> It's what you can prove.</p>
282	        </section>
283	        <NecessaryConditionExperience/>
284	        <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation" aria-label="Why a requirement is not necessarily enough">
285	          <h3>Two roles in the same rule</h3>
286	          <p>Compare the two roles: <strong>being enrolled is enough to prove being a student; being a student is required for being enrolled.</strong> These describe the same rule from different sides.</p>
287	          <p>Whenever the sufficient condition is true, the necessary condition must be true too. The rule does not tell us that either condition is actually true of a particular person.</p>
288	          <details className="cr-extension"><summary>Can a condition be both necessary and sufficient?</summary><p>Yes, when the rule supports both directions. Necessary means required; it doesn't mean never enough. Our rule tells us that enrollment requires being a student. It doesn't say that being a student is enough to prove enrollment.</p></details>
289	        </section>
290	        <SufficientConditionFilm key="necessary-definition" assets={necessaryConditionFilms} title="Why the requirement is not enough here" ariaLabel="Short explanation of necessity"/>
291	        <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation sc-indicator-list" aria-label="Group 2 indicator words">
292	          <h3>Group 2: Necessary-condition indicators</h3>
293	          <p>In an if-then rule, the necessary condition is the part after <strong>then</strong>. These words and phrases mark that same part, wherever it sits in the sentence. Each one tells you a <strong>necessary condition</strong> is in play. Read the whole rule, then identify <strong>what is required and what requires it</strong>.</p>
294	          <NecessaryIndicatorExplorer/>
295	          <h3>Why only if is different from if</h3>
296	          <div className="nc-role-comparison">
297	            <div><h4>If you are enrolled in law school, you are a student.</h4><p><strong>If</strong> identifies enrollment as enough. This is our original rule.</p></div>
298	            <div><h4>You are enrolled in law school only if you are a student.</h4><p><strong>Only if</strong> identifies being a student as required. This keeps that same rule.</p></div>
299	          </div>
300	          <p>Now remove only from the second sentence: “You are enrolled in law school <strong>if</strong> you are a student.” That would make being a student enough for law-school enrollment. A history student who is not enrolled in law school shows why that is a different claim.</p>
301	          <p>Ask <strong>what the rule requires</strong>. Identify the complete condition, including any restrictions. The next example gives you a new rule to check.</p>
302	        </section>
303	        <section className="cr-sufficient-explanation nc-checks" aria-label="Apply the necessary condition to a new rule">
304	          <h3>Find the requirement in a new rule</h3>
305	          <blockquote>An application is accepted for review only if it includes a current reference letter.</blockquote>
306	          <ReferenceQuestion prompt="What is required for an application to be accepted for review?" options={['Including a current reference letter.', 'Including any reference letter, even an outdated one.', 'Being accepted for review.']} correct={0} feedback={['Only if identifies including a current reference letter as required. An application accepted for review cannot be missing that current letter.', 'Current matters. An outdated letter does not meet the requirement in this rule. Keep that word when you identify what is required.', 'Acceptance is the condition that requires the letter. Including a current reference letter is the requirement. You have swapped the two roles.']}/>
307	          <h3>Does meeting the requirement guarantee acceptance?</h3>
308	          <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Does including a current reference letter guarantee that the application is accepted for review?" options={['Yes. The letter is all the application needs.', 'No. The letter is required, but the rule does not say it is enough.']} correct={1} feedback={['You turned a requirement into a guarantee. The rule tells us that accepted applications include a current letter, not that every application with one is accepted.', 'An application with a current letter could be accepted or not accepted. Both fit the rule. Having the required letter alone does not prove acceptance.']}/>
309	        </section>
310	        <aside className="cr-sufficient-bridge"><h3>Next: Put the rule into a diagram</h3><p>We now have both roles: the condition that guarantees, and the condition it requires. Next, we'll use an arrow to record that connection.</p></aside>
311	      </div>
312	      <p>Next, record the rule with the must arrow.</p>
313	    </ReferenceStep>
314	
315	    <ReferenceStep number={5} label="The necessary family" title="Listen for what the rule needs">
316	      <p>Return to our locker rule. Borrowing a locker requires a key. The next indicators all identify that requirement in their complete sentences.</p>
317	      <WordFamily entries={necessaryFamily} role="necessary"/>

11	type ContentSection = ReactElement<{ id?: string; number?: number; title?: string; children?: ReactNode; dark?: boolean; 'data-reference-boundary'?: boolean }>;

13	// Compose the approved source sections directly. Their stateful exercises are
14	// mounted by React; the former reader-page renderer is never invoked here.
15	function sections(children: ReactNode): ContentSection[] {
16	  return Children.toArray(children).flatMap(child => {
17	    if (!isValidElement<ContentSection['props']>(child)) return [];
18	    return child.type === Fragment ? sections(child.props.children) : [child];
19	  });
20	}

22	function reference(number: number): ContentSection {
23	  const section = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(item => item.props.number === number);
24	  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional reference section ${number}`);
25	  return section;
26	}
27	
28	function groupSections(group: 'group-1' | 'group-2') {
29	  return sections(GroupLessons({ id: group }).props.children);
30	}

32	function groupSection(group: 'group-1' | 'group-2', title: string) {
33	  const section = groupSections(group).find(item => item.props.id === title || item.props.title === title);
34	  if (!section) throw new Error(`Missing approved conditional section: ${title}`);
35	  const sectionId = `course-${group}-${title.toLowerCase().replace(/[^a-z0-9]+/g, '-')}`;
36	  return cloneElement(section, { key: sectionId, id: sectionId });
37	}

41	}

64	// Only and only if both mark the requirement, from different places in the rule.
65	function OnlyAndOnlyIf() {
66	  return <NoteSection title="Only and only if can mark the same requirement">
67	    <div className="cr-language-pair"><p><strong>Only</strong> [people with a key] borrow a locker.</p><p>A person borrows a locker <strong>only if</strong> [they have a key].</p><span>Only and only if identify the necessary condition here.</span></div>
68	    <p>Both sentences say locker borrowers have a key. In the first, only is at the beginning. In the second, only if is in the middle. Having a key is required in both, so it goes right. Read only if as the whole phrase; the if inside it isn't doing the job if does on its own.</p>

Which condition goes on the right?" options={['Borrows a locker', 'Has a key']} correct={1} feedback={['Borrowing is what has the requirement. Ask what a locker borrower must have: a key. Borrowing goes left; having a key goes right.', 'Having a key is required for borrowing. Only if appears in the middle, but it still identifies the necessary condition.']}/>
70	  </NoteSection>;

99	function ContrapositiveNote() {
100	  return <>
101	    <NoteSection title="What a contrapositive is">
102	      <p>If the sufficient condition happens, the necessary condition <strong>must</strong> happen. The left side of the arrow guarantees the right side.</p>
103	      <p>The contrapositive is what you know when the right side of the arrow goes missing:</p>
104	      <p className="ct-definition"><strong>If the <span className="ct-hl">necessary condition</span> is absent, the <span className="ct-hl" data-tone="amber">sufficient condition</span> is absent too.</strong></p>
105	      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the ceiling light is on, then the power must be on.</strong> With no power, the light can't be on. No power, no light. As a rule: <strong>If the power is off, then the ceiling light must be off.</strong></p>
106	    </NoteSection>
107	    <NoteSection title="You already knew this">
108	      <p>The contrapositive was inside the original rule the whole time, waiting for you to spot it. That's why the two are <PulseWord>logically equivalent</PulseWord>: if one is true, the other is true, and you can use either. They're <PunchWord>interchangeable!</PunchWord></p>
109	    </NoteSection>
110	    <NoteSection title="Watch the lesson">
111	      <ContrapositiveLecture/>
112	    </NoteSection>
113	    <NoteSection title="How to write it">
114	      <p>Write the rule with an arrow: <strong>Light → Power</strong>. The sufficient condition goes on the left side of the arrow. The necessary condition goes on the right side of the arrow.</p>
115	      <p>The contrapositive is <strong><Neg>Power</Neg> → <Neg>Light</Neg></strong>. A line through an idea means “not.” You get there in two steps:</p>
116	      <ol className="ct-moves">
117	        <li><strong>Step 1: Flip the sides.</strong> The right side goes on the left, and the left side goes on the right.</li>
118	        <li><strong>Step 2: Negate both.</strong> Put “not” on each idea. If an idea already has a “not,” the two cancel: a double negative turns positive.</li>
119	      </ol>
120	      <FlipAndNegateDemo/>
121	      <p>Same two steps when a side already has a “not.” Watch what happens to it:</p>
122	      <DoubleNegativeDemo/>
123	    </NoteSection>
124	    <NoteSection title="What it is not">
125	      <p>Two look-alikes fail. Wrong answer choices use them on purpose.</p>
126	      <ThreeMoves/>
127	    </NoteSection>
128	    <NoteSection title="Read it, don't always write it">
129	      <p>You can read a rule backward without writing anything: go from right to left and put a “not” on everything. Write the contrapositive when it helps you see the rule or connect it to another rule.</p>
130	    </NoteSection>
131	  </>;
132	}

169	// Teach placement and reading through the familiar relationship, then require a decision.
170	function MustArrowNote() {
171	  return <section className="co-section cr-course-section ma-lesson" data-reference-source={8}>
172	    <div className="co-section-heading"><h2>What the arrow says</h2></div>
173	    <div className="cr-step-body">
174	      <p className="ma-lead">An arrow joins the two conditions in a rule. It says one thing: <strong>if the condition on the left is true, the condition on the right must be true.</strong></p>
175	      <figure className="ma-example" aria-label="If you are enrolled in law school, you must be a student">
176	        <figcaption className="ma-read-instruction">Read it as a sentence: <strong>“If you are enrolled in law school, then you must be a student.”</strong></figcaption>
177	        <div className="ma-relationship">
178	          <div className="ma-condition"><span className="ma-side"><small>Left side</small>Sufficient condition</span><strong>You are enrolled in law school</strong><span className="ma-meaning">Enough to guarantee the right side</span></div>
179	          <div className="ma-arrow" aria-hidden="true"><span>must</span><svg viewBox="0 0 100 32" fill="none"><path d="M3 16H93M77 3L94 16L77 29" stroke="currentColor" strokeWidth="5" strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round"/></svg></div>
180	          <div className="ma-condition"><span className="ma-side"><small>Right side</small>Necessary condition</span><strong>You are a student</strong><span className="ma-meaning">Required when the left side is true</span></div>
181	        </div>
182	      </figure>
183	      <p>Being enrolled in law school is <strong>enough</strong> to know you are a student. That is why enrollment goes on the <strong>left</strong>. Being a student is <strong>required</strong> for enrollment, so it goes on the <strong>right</strong>. The arrow points from what is enough to what is required.</p>
184	      <section className="ma-where" aria-label="Where to start reading">
185	        <h3>Start at the left</h3>
186	        <p>A rule begins where the arrow begins. Say “if” and read the left condition. Then say “then” and read the right one: <strong>If you are enrolled in law school, then you are a student.</strong></p>
187	        <p>Reading from the right says something else. “You are a student, so you are enrolled” is not what this rule says. The arrow only runs one way, and you start at its tail.</p>
188	      </section>
189	      <section className="ma-purpose" aria-label="What the arrow is for">
190	        <h3>What the arrow is for</h3>
191	        <p>A rule can take a full sentence to say. The arrow shows what it promises in one line, so you can test a fact against it without rereading the sentence.</p>
192	        <ul>
193	          <li>You learn the <strong>left</strong> side is true. The right side must be true too.</li>
194	          <li>You learn the <strong>right</strong> side is true. The left side stays open.</li>
195	        </ul>
196	        <p>This matters on the LSAT because arguments turn on which way a rule runs. A conclusion that follows the arrow is supported. A conclusion that runs against it needs something the rule never gave you.</p>
197	      </section>
198	      <section className="ma-placement" aria-label="Place and read the conditions">
199	        <h3>Place the conditions. Then read the arrow.</h3>
200	        <p>Choose what belongs on each side, then use the same direction with a new rule.</p>
201	        <p className="ma-compact-note">Put the <strong>condition</strong> beside the arrow, not the indicator word. For example, write “you win the raffle,” not “if.”</p>
202	        <MustArrowPractice />
203	      </section>
204	      <section className="ma-order" aria-label="Two ways to say the same thing">
205	        <h3>Two ways to say the same thing</h3>
206	        <div className="cr-language-pair"><p><strong>If</strong> you win the raffle, you get a bicycle.</p><p>You get a bicycle <strong>if</strong> you win the raffle.</p></div>
207	        <p>Both mean: <strong>You win the raffle → You get a bicycle.</strong> Winning is sufficient, so it goes left, even when it comes last in the rule.</p>
208	      </section>
209	      <p className="ma-takeaway"><strong>The arrow guarantees the right side when the left side is true.</strong> Being a student alone does not prove that you are enrolled in law school.</p>
210	      <details className="cr-extension ma-reverse"><summary>Why can't we reverse it?</summary><div className="ma-reverse-body"><p>A person could be a history student who is not enrolled in law school. That possibility fits our rule.</p><p>The arrow states a rule. By itself, it does not tell us that either condition is true, or that one causes the other.</p></div></details>
211	    </div>
212	  </section>;
213	}

215	// One translation process, checked four ways, instead of five restarted lessons.
216	function WholeMeaningNote() {
217	  const onlyComparison = groupSection('group-2', 'Compare only with the only');
218	  const insideCheck = Children.toArray(reference(12).props.children);
219	  return <>
220	    <NoteSection title="Check a translation four ways">
221	      <p>A diagram can point the right way and still say the wrong thing. Each check below catches a different slip in the same translation process.</p>
222	      <ol>
223	        <li><strong>Target:</strong> did you find the whole idea the indicator refers to, wherever it sits?</li>
224	        <li><strong>Qualification:</strong> did your short label keep every restriction?</li>
225	        <li><strong>Direction:</strong> did a similar-looking indicator trick you into the wrong side?</li>
226	        <li><strong>Imagined case:</strong> does your arrow promise something the rule leaves open?</li>
227	      </ol>
228	    </NoteSection>
229	    <NoteSection title="Check 1: find the whole group, wherever it sits">
230	      <p>An indicator's <strong>target</strong> is the complete word or phrase it refers to. In “All guests wearing blue badges receive vouchers,” all refers to guests wearing blue badges, not guests alone.</p>
231	      <p>The group often comes after its indicator. But often isn't always. Try these rules in order.</p>
232	      {targetExamples.map(item => <TargetReader item={item} key={item.label}/>)}
233	      <div className="cr-language-pair"><p>[Having a key] is <strong>required</strong> for borrowing a locker.</p><p>Borrowing a locker <strong>requires</strong> [having a key].</p><span>Having a key is the requirement in both sentences.</span></div>
234	      <p>The group comes before required in the first sentence and after requires in the second. Having a key still goes right. Neither the closest noun nor the next word is a reliable group rule. Ask which complete idea the rule calls required, then give that idea its role.</p>
235	    </NoteSection>
236	    <NoteSection title="Check 2: keep every qualification">
237	      <p>Once you have the group, shorten it without losing what limits it. These two sentences test the same habit from opposite sides of the arrow.</p>
238	      <div className="co-word-comparison">
239	        <div><blockquote>Any applicant who submits late pays a fee.</blockquote><p>Applicant alone isn't the condition. The rule is about an applicant who submits late. Drop that restriction and your diagram makes a claim about on-time applicants too.</p></div>
240	        <div><blockquote>Entry requires a current pass.</blockquote><p>A current pass is required. Has a pass drops current and would include an expired pass. A shorter label should save writing, not change the requirement.</p></div>
241	      </div>
242	      <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Any applicant who submits late pays a fee. What goes left?" options={['Applicant', 'Applicant submits late']} correct={1} feedback={['You dropped the late-submission restriction. Applicant alone would cover every applicant, including someone who submits on time. The sufficient idea is applicant submits late.', 'This keeps the complete sufficient idea. The rule covers applicants who submit late, so keep that restriction with the applicant.']}/>
243	      <ReferenceQuestion prompt="Entry requires a current pass. What goes right?" options={['Has current pass', 'Has any pass']} correct={0} feedback={['This keeps the full requirement. 298	/**
299	 * Spot the condition saves answers by position. Order 1 put the concept and
300	 * indicator questions first and the rule-vs-fact questions last; order 2 puts
301	 * the rule-vs-fact questions first. Map each current position to its order 1
302	 * position so earlier answers stay with the question they answered.
303	 */
304	const FOUNDATION_ORDER = 2;
305	function foundationLegacyOrder(conceptCount: number, ruleCount: number): Record<number, number[]> {
306	  return { 1: [...Array.from({ length: ruleCount }, (_, index) => conceptCount + index), ...Array.from({ length: conceptCount }, (_, index) => index)] };
307	}

317	  const anchorQuestion = anchor as ReactElement<DrillOneQuestion>;

321	    { ...freshCheck.props, rule: 'Every delivery marked collected has a pickup receipt.', prompt: 'What is sufficient here?' },
322	    { rule: 'If you win the raffle, you get a bicycle.', prompt: 'Someone claims that getting a bicycle proves you won the raffle. Which possible case fits our rule but disproves that claim?', options: ['You win and get a bicycle.', 'You do not win, but get a bicycle as a gift.', 'You win and never get a bicycle.'], correct: 1, feedback: ['This fits both the rule and the claim, so it cannot disprove the claim. We need a case where the rule holds but the claim does not.', 'The rule promises a bicycle to a winner. 323	  ];

328	    ['A student goes on the museum trip', 'That student has a signed permission slip'],

339	function PermissionBoundary() {
340	  const boundary = sections(conditionalReferenceContent().props.children).find(section => section.props['data-reference-boundary'] !== undefined);
341	  if (!boundary) throw new Error('Missing approved permission and instruction boundary');

345	function Content({ id }: { id: string }) {
346	  switch (id) {
347	    case 'meaning': return <ReferenceBody number={1} omitTransition/>;
348	    case 'sufficient': return <ReferenceBody number={2} omitTransition/>;
349	    case 'necessary': return <ReferenceBody number={4} omitTransition/>;
350	    case 'must-arrow': return <MustArrowNote/>;
351	    case 'sufficient-words': return <><IndicatorNote group="group-1"/><CompleteConditionNote/></>;
352	    case 'necessary-words': return <><IndicatorNote group="group-2"/><PermissionBoundary/></>;
353	    case 'whole-meaning': return <WholeMeaningNote/>;
354	    case 'contrapositive': return <><ContrapositiveNote/><FourRules/></>;
355	    case 'four-situations': return <><FourSituationsNote/><section className="co-section"><div className="co-section-heading"><PopQuizTitle/></div><FourSituationsQuiz/></section></>;
356	    case 'foundation-practice': return <FoundationPractice/>;
357	    case 'sufficient-practice': return <StagedReasoningDrill mode="sufficient"/>;
358	    case 'mixed-practice': return <StagedReasoningDrill mode="mixed"/>;
359	    case 'contrapositive-practice': return <StagedReasoningDrill mode="contrapositive"/>;
360	    default: throw new Error(`Unknown opening course content: ${id}`);
361	  }
362	}

364	/** Article body only. Course routes own titles, navigation and completion. */
365	export function OpeningCourseContent({ id }: { id: string }) {

export type OpeningCourseStep = {
  id: string;
  type: 'note' | 'drill';
  title: string;
  description: string;
  alsoAvailableIn?: Array<'orientation' | 'sufficient-necessary' | 'group-1' | 'group-2'>;
  unitId: 'orientation' | 'sufficient-necessary' | 'group-1' | 'group-2';
};

// Reading destinations are not legacy completion IDs. Visiting one records no completion.
export const OPENING_COURSE_PATH: OpeningCourseStep[] = [
  { id: 'meaning', type: 'note', title: 'What conditionals are', description: 'Separate a rule from a fact, and see what a conditional actually promises.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'sufficient', type: 'note', title: 'The Sufficient Condition', description: 'When one condition guarantees another.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'necessary', type: 'note', title: 'The Necessary Condition', description: 'What the sufficient condition requires.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'must-arrow', type: 'note', title: 'Must arrow', description: 'What the arrow says, where to start reading it, and what it is for.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'foundation-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Spot the condition', description: 'Identify sufficient and necessary conditions, place them beside the arrow, recognize indicators, and decide what the facts prove.', unitId: 'sufficient-necessary', alsoAvailableIn: ['orientation'] },
  { id: 'sufficient-words', type: 'note', title: 'Find the complete condition', description: 'Identify the full sufficient condition, including the words that limit who or what the rule covers.', unitId: 'group-1' },
  { id: 'sufficient-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Build the sufficient side', description: 'Find the whole condition, construct the diagram and repair its direction.', unitId: 'group-1' },
  { id: 'necessary-words', type: 'note', title: 'Necessary-condition words', description: 'Read only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires and must.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'whole-meaning', type: 'note', title: 'Keep the whole meaning', description: 'Keep qualifications, permission and obligation intact before translating a rule.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-intro-identify', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 2: Find both conditions', description: 'Find the indicators and their groups, then write the complete sufficient and necessary conditions.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-roles', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 3: Place the conditions', description: 'Put each condition on its side of the arrow.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-intro-diagrams', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 4: Build the diagram', description: 'Return to the introductory sentences and build each diagram.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-rephrase', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 5: Write it as if/then', description: 'Rewrite each rule as if/then without changing its meaning, then compare your wording with the explanation.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'mixed-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Conditional translation practice', description: 'Who or what does the rule cover? What does it require? Answer without adding anything the rule does not say.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'contrapositive', type: 'note', title: 'Contrapositive', description: 'What it is, how to write it, and what it is not.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'four-situations', type: 'note', title: 'What the rule tells you', description: 'Four situations: two settle something, two settle nothing.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'source-intro-diagrams-contrapositive', type: 'drill', title: 'Revisit Drill 4: Add the contrapositive', description: 'Add the contrapositive to the rules you already know.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-rephrase-contrapositive', type: 'drill', title: 'Revisit Drill 5: Negate the whole condition', description: 'Complete the contrapositive for each rewritten rule. Keep every qualification.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'contrapositive-practice', type: 'drill', title: 'Check your contrapositive', description: 'Choose the correct transformation, including rules that already contain negative conditions.', unitId: 'orientation' },
  { id: 'source-full-translation', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 6: Build both diagrams', description: 'Build the original and the contrapositive. Keep each complete condition intact.', unitId: 'group-2' },
  { id: 'source-independent', type: 'drill', title: 'Drill 7: Put it into practice', description: 'Run the full process on your own. Work in short sessions and return where you left off.', unitId: 'group-2' },
];

export function openingCourseStep(id?: string) {
  return OPENING_COURSE_PATH.find(step => step.id === id);
}

// Alternatives name legacy units that already teach this complete concept.
export function openingStepAccessUnits(step: OpeningCourseStep) {
  return [step.unitId, ...(step.alsoAvailableIn ?? [])];
}

18	/** The four-situation chart. Rows rise in one after another when the chart scrolls into view;
19	 *  the two that settle something get a green check, the two that settle nothing go quiet. */
20	function SituationChart() {
21	  const ref = useRef<HTMLTableElement>(null);
22	  const [inView, setInView] = useState(typeof IntersectionObserver === 'undefined');
23	  useEffect(() => {
24	    const node = ref.current;
25	    if (!node || typeof IntersectionObserver === 'undefined') return;
26	    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(entries => { if (entries.some(entry => entry.isIntersecting)) { setInView(true); observer.disconnect(); } }, { threshold: 0.3 });
27	    observer.observe(node);
28	    return () => observer.disconnect();
29	  }, []);
30	  return <table ref={ref} className="fs-chart" data-in-view={inView}>
31	    <caption className="fs-chart-rule"><span>The rule</span>If the pool is open, then a lifeguard is on duty.</caption>
32	    <thead><tr><th scope="col">Situation</th><th scope="col">What follows?</th><th scope="col">The pool rule</th></tr></thead>
33	    <tbody>
34	      {situations.map((row, index) => <tr key={row.key} data-settled={row.settled} style={{ animationDelay: `${index * 0.14}s` }}>
35	        <th scope="row" data-label="Situation"><span className="fs-situation"><Pill text={row.idea} tone={row.tone} negated={false}/>{' '}<span>{row.present ? 'present' : 'absent'}</span></span></th>
36	        <td data-label="What follows?"><span className="fs-follows">{row.settled ? <Check size={18} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/> : <Minus size={18} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/>}{row.follows}</span></td>
37	        <td data-label="The pool rule"><span className="fs-tag">{row.tag}</span><span className="fs-example">{row.example}</span></td>
38	      </tr>)}
39	    </tbody>
40	  </table>;
41	}

94	export function FourSituationsNote() {
95	  return <>
96	    <Section title="All a rule gives you">
97	      <p className="ct-definition"><strong>A conditional tells you two things: the original rule and its contrapositive. That is all it tells you.</strong></p>
98	      <p>Take this rule: <strong>If the pool is open, then a lifeguard is on duty.</strong></p>
99	      <p>The original: if the pool is open, a lifeguard must be on duty. The contrapositive: <strong>if no lifeguard is on duty, the pool can’t be open.</strong> The rule says nothing else.</p>
100	    </Section>
101	    <Section title="Don’t fall for the necessary condition">
102	      <p>The LSAT will give you a rule, then tell you the necessary condition is present. A lifeguard is on duty! You’ll want to conclude the pool is open. Don’t. The lifeguard could be there for a training session with the pool closed.</p>
103	      <p>When the necessary condition is present, <PulseWord>the rule tells you nothing.</PulseWord> Set it aside.</p>
104	    </Section>
105	    <Section title="Four situations">
106	      <p>Any fact about a rule puts you in one of four situations. Two of them settle something. Two settle nothing.</p>
107	      <SituationChart/>
108	    </Section>
109	    <Section title="Ask where the fact lands">
110	      <p>When a rule comes with a fact, check which side the fact is about. Sufficient present or necessary absent, and the rule settles something. Sufficient absent or necessary present, and it settles nothing, so look for help somewhere else in the argument.</p>
111	    </Section>
112	  </>;
113	}

60	/** One question at a time, one tap each. A wrong tap says "Not that one." and why; the right tap gives the answer as a plain sentence. */
61	export function QuickQuiz({ items, idPrefix }: { items: QuizItem[]; idPrefix: string }) {
62	  const [step, setStep] = useState(0);
63	  const [wrong, setWrong] = useState<number[]>([]);
64	  const [solved, setSolved] = useState<number | null>(null);
65	  const item = items[step];
66	  const last = step === items.length - 1;
67	  const lastWrong = wrong.length ? item.options[wrong[wrong.length - 1]] : null;
68	  const message = solved !== null ? item.answer : lastWrong ? `Not that one. ${lastWrong.reason}` : 'Pick the answer the rule supports.';
69	  const next = () => { setStep(value => value + 1); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };
70	  const restart = () => { setStep(0); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };
71	  return <div className="ct-drill" data-step={step}>
72	    <div className="ct-drill-top">
73	      <p className="ct-picker-kicker">Your turn</p>
74	      <p className="ct-drill-count">Question {step + 1} of {items.length}<span className="ct-drill-dots" aria-hidden="true">{items.map((entry, index) => <i key={index} data-state={index < step ? 'done' : index === step ? 'now' : 'todo'}/>)}</span></p>
75	    </div>
76	    <p className="ct-drill-sentence">{item.rule}</p>
77	    <p className="ct-picker-question" id={`${idPrefix}-q-${step}`}>{item.question}</p>
78	    <div className="ct-drill-options" role="group" aria-labelledby={`${idPrefix}-q-${step}`}>
79	      {item.options.map((option, index) => {
80	        const state = solved === index ? 'right' : wrong.includes(index) ? 'wrong' : 'open';
81	        return <button key={option.text} type="button" className="ct-drill-option" data-state={state} disabled={solved !== null && solved !== index} onClick={() => { if (solved !== null) return; if (index === item.correct) setSolved(index); else setWrong(list => list.includes(index) ? list : [...list, index]); }}>
82	          {option.tone ? <Pill text={option.text} tone={option.tone} negated={false}/> : <span className="sq-option-text">{option.text}</span>}
83	          {state === 'right' && <span className="ct-drill-mark" data-kind="right"><Check size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/>Yes</span>}
84	          {state === 'wrong' && <span className="ct-drill-mark" data-kind="wrong"><X size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/>No</span>}
85	        </button>;
86	      })}
87	    </div>
88	    <p className="ct-picker-message" role="status" data-solved={solved !== null}>{message}</p>
89	    {solved !== null && (last
90	      ? <div className="ct-drill-end"><strong>That is all four.</strong><button type="button" className="ct-drill-next" onClick={restart}><RotateCcw size={15} aria-hidden="true"/>Try them again</button></div>
91	      : <button type="button" className="ct-drill-next" onClick={next}>Next question</button>)}
92	  </div>;
93	}

/** Keeps the rule readable while drawing attention to its complete required clause. */
export function NecessaryConditionStatement() {
  return <figure className="nc-statement">
    <blockquote>
      <span className="nc-statement-if">If you are enrolled in law school,</span>{' '}
      <strong className="nc-statement-then nc-statement-emphasis">
        then you are a student.
      </strong>
    </blockquote>
    <figcaption>
      <div className="nc-statement-roles">
        <div><span>Sufficient condition</span><strong>You are enrolled in law school</strong><p>Enough to prove that you are a student.</p></div>
        <div><span>Necessary condition</span><strong>You are a student</strong><p>Required whenever you are enrolled in law school.</p></div>
      </div>
    </figcaption>
  </figure>;
}

So this person must be a student.' },
  { label: 'History student', fact: 'Suppose this person is a history student who is not enrolled in law school.', student: 'Is a student', studentNote: 'Given in this example', enrollment: 'Not enrolled', note: 'Given in this example', title: 'Being a student does not guarantee law-school enrollment.', explanation: 'This person is a student, but is not enrolled in law school. That fits the rule. Being a student is required for enrollment, but it is not enough to prove enrollment.' },
  { label: 'Not a student', fact: 'Suppose this person is not a student.', student: 'Not a student', studentNote: 'Given in this example', enrollment: 'Ruled out', note: 'Follows from the rule', title: 'Without being a student, this person cannot be enrolled in law school.', explanation: 'Anyone enrolled in law school has to be a student. Because this person is not a student, enrollment is ruled out. The requirement cannot be false while enrollment is true.' },
] as const;

export function NecessaryConditionExperience() {
  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);
  const tabs = useRef<(HTMLButtonElement | null)[]>([]);
  const id = useId();
  function navigateTabs(event: KeyboardEvent<HTMLButtonElement>, index: number) {
    let next: number;
    switch (event.key) {
      case 'ArrowDown': next = (index + 1) % cases.length; break;
      case 'ArrowUp': next = (index + cases.length - 1) % cases.length; break;
      case 'Home': next = 0; break;
      case 'End': next = cases.length - 1; break;
      default: return;
    }
    event.preventDefault();
    setSelected(next);
    tabs.current[next]?.focus();
  }
  return <section className="nc-experience" aria-labelledby={`${id}-heading`}>
    <header>
      <h3 id={`${id}-heading`}>What does each case tell you?</h3>
      <div className="nc-case-rule"><span>The rule</span><p>If you are enrolled in law school, then you are a student.</p></div>
    </header>
    <div className="nc-tab-layout">
      <div className="nc-person-tabs" role="tablist" aria-label="Choose a person to compare" aria-orientation="vertical">
        {cases.map((item, index) => <button key={item.label} ref={(element) => { tabs.current[index] = element; }} type="button" role="tab" id={`${id}-tab-${index}`} aria-selected={selected === index} tabIndex={selected === index ? 0 : -1} aria-controls={`${id}-panel-${index}`} onKeyDown={(event) => navigateTabs(event, index)} onClick={() => setSelected(index)}>{item.label}</button>)}
      </div>
      <div className="nc-person-panels">
        {cases.map((item, index) => <div key={item.label} className="nc-person-panel" id={`${id}-panel-${index}`} role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby={`${id}-tab-${index}`} tabIndex={0} hidden={selected !== index}>
          <p className="nc-person-fact">{item.fact}</p>
          <dl className="nc-person-facts">
            <div><dt>Being a student</dt><dd><strong>{item.student}</strong><span>{item.studentNote}</span></dd></div>
            <div><dt>Law-school enrollment</dt><dd><strong>{item.enrollment}</strong><span>{item.note}</span></dd></div>
          </dl>
          <div className="nc-reading">
            <h4>{item.title}</h4>
            <p>{item.explanation}</p>
          </div>
        </div>)}
      </div>
    </div>
  </section>;
}

import './necessary-indicator-explorer.css';

const examples = [
  { expression: 'Only', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote><strong>Only</strong> students are enrolled in law school.</blockquote><p>This excludes nonstudents from the law-school group. It does not put every student in that group. Being a student remains required; being enrolled remains enough to prove it.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Only if', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>You are enrolled in law school <strong>only if</strong> you are a student.</blockquote><p>Only if introduces the requirement: <strong>you are a student</strong>. Read the whole phrase. The if inside only if does not make being a student sufficient.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Only when', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>You are enrolled in law school <strong>only when</strong> you are a student.</blockquote><p>On any occasion when enrollment is true, being a student must be true too. This does not say you become enrolled whenever you are a student, or that one condition has to happen later than the other.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Only where', necessaryCondition: 'A tutor is present where the seminar takes place.', content: <><p>This indicator restricts places, so use a setting example:</p><blockquote>A seminar takes place <strong>only where</strong> a tutor is present.</blockquote><p>At any place with that seminar, <strong>a tutor must be present</strong>. A tutor could also be somewhere without a seminar. A seminar taking place is sufficient to prove a tutor's presence there; the tutor's presence is necessary for the seminar.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Always', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>While you are enrolled in law school, you are <strong>always</strong> a student.</blockquote><p>Always allows no exception within the situation described: being enrolled in law school. It does not say every student is enrolled, or that someone remains a student after enrollment ends.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Requires', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>Being enrolled in law school <strong>requires</strong> being a student.</blockquote><p>What does enrollment require? <strong>Being a student.</strong> Requires is the indicator; being a student is the condition it identifies. They are not the same thing.</p></> },
  { expression: 'Must', necessaryCondition: 'You are a student.', content: <><blockquote>If you are enrolled in law school, you <strong>must</strong> be a student.</blockquote><p>Must makes the certainty explicit: enrollment cannot coexist with not being a student. Here it describes what has to be true, not a task someone has been told to complete.</p></> },
];

export function NecessaryIndicatorExplorer() {
  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);
  const tabs = useRef<(HTMLButtonElement | null)[]>([]);
  const id = useId();
  function navigate(event: KeyboardEvent<HTMLButtonElement>, index: number) {
    let next: number;
    switch (event.key) {
      case 'ArrowDown': case 'ArrowRight': next = (index + 1) % examples.length; break;
      case 'ArrowUp': case 'ArrowLeft': next = (index + examples.length - 1) % examples.length; break;
      case 'Home': next = 0; break;
      case 'End': next = examples.length - 1; break;
      default: return;
    }
    event.preventDefault();
    setSelected(next);
    tabs.current[next]?.focus();
  }
  return <div className="ni-explorer">
    <p className="ni-instruction">Choose an indicator to see the necessary condition it identifies in a complete sentence.</p>
    <div className="ni-layout">
      <ol className="ni-expression-list" role="tablist" aria-label="Necessary-condition indicators" aria-orientation="vertical">
        {examples.map((example, index) => <li key={example.expression} role="presentation">
          <button type="button" role="tab" id={`${id}-tab-${index}`} aria-selected={selected === index}
            tabIndex={selected === index ? 0 : -1} aria-controls={`${id}-example-${index}`}
            ref={element => { tabs.current[index] = element; }} onKeyDown={event => navigate(event, index)}
            onPointerEnter={event => {
              if (event.pointerType === 'mouse' && window.matchMedia('(hover: hover) and (min-width: 621px)').matches) setSelected(index);
            }}
            onFocus={() => setSelected(index)} onClick={() => setSelected(index)}>
            <span className="ni-number" aria-hidden="true">{String(index + 1).padStart(2, '0')}</span>
            <span>{example.expression.toLowerCase()}</span><span className="ni-indicator" aria-hidden="true">↗</span>
          </button>
        </li>)}
      </ol>
      <div className="ni-examples">
        {examples.map((example, index) => <section key={example.expression} id={`${id}-example-${index}`}
          role="tabpanel" aria-labelledby={`${id}-tab-${index}`} tabIndex={0} hidden={selected !== index} className="ni-example">
          <p className="ni-example-label">{String(index + 1).padStart(2, '0')} / {String(examples.length).padStart(2, '0')} · The rule and its meaning</p>
          <dl className="nc-indicator-examples"><div><dt>{example.expression}</dt><dd>{example.content}</dd></div></dl>
          <div className="ni-requirement"><span>Necessary condition</span><strong>{example.necessaryCondition}</strong></div>
        </section>)}
      </div>
    </div>
  </div>;
}

import { useId, useState } from 'react';
import { sufficientConditionFilms } from '../content/sufficientConditionFilms';

export type SufficientConditionFilmAsset = { id: string; title: string; src: string; poster?: string; captions: string; caption: string; transcript: string };

/** Render only an actual generated asset; the written lesson remains available without video. */
export function SufficientConditionFilm({ assets = sufficientConditionFilms, title = 'What the rule gives us', ariaLabel = 'Short explanations of sufficiency' }: { assets?: SufficientConditionFilmAsset[]; title?: string; ariaLabel?: string }) {
  const [selected, setSelected] = useState(0);
  const [failedId, setFailedId] = useState<string | null>(null);
  const [attempt, setAttempt] = useState(0);
  const videoId = useId();
  const asset = assets[selected];
  if (!asset) return null;
  const failed = failedId === asset.id;
  return <section className="sc-film" aria-label={ariaLabel}>
    <header><p className="sc-eyebrow">Watch the explanation</p><h3>{title}</h3></header>
    {assets.length > 1 && <div className="sc-film-selector" role="group" aria-label="Choose an explanation">
      {assets.map((clip, index) => <button type="button" key={clip.id}
        aria-pressed={index === selected} aria-controls={videoId}
        onClick={() => { setSelected(index); setFailedId(null); }}>{clip.title}</button>)}
    </div>}
    <figure>
      <video id={videoId} key={`${asset.id}-${attempt}`} controls preload="metadata" playsInline
        poster={asset.poster} aria-label={asset.caption} onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}>
        <source src={asset.src} type="video/mp4" onError={() => setFailedId(asset.id)}/>
        <track kind="captions" src={asset.captions} srcLang="en" label="English" default/>
        Your browser cannot play this clip. The explanation is available below.
      </video>
      <figcaption>{asset.caption}</figcaption>
    </figure>
    {failed && <div className="sc-film-error">
      <p role="alert">The video couldn't load. You can read the explanation below or try again.</p>
      <button type="button" onClick={() => { setFailedId(null); setAttempt(value => value + 1); }}>Try loading the video again</button>
    </div>}
    <details key={`transcript-${asset.id}-${failed}`} open={failed || undefined}>
      <summary>Read the video explanation</summary><p>{asset.transcript}</p>
    </details>
  </section>;
}

5	/** True once the element has scrolled into view. Without IntersectionObserver (old browsers, tests) it is true straight away. */
6	function useInView<T extends Element>(): [React.RefObject<T>, boolean] {
7	  const ref = useRef<T>(null);
8	  const [inView, setInView] = useState(typeof IntersectionObserver === 'undefined');
9	  useEffect(() => {
10	    const node = ref.current;
11	    if (!node || typeof IntersectionObserver === 'undefined') return;
12	    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(entries => { if (entries.some(entry => entry.isIntersecting)) { setInView(true); observer.disconnect(); } }, { threshold: 0.6 });
13	    observer.observe(node);
14	    return () => observer.disconnect();
15	  }, []);
16	  return [ref, inView];
17	}

100	/** The four possible worlds. The student taps the one the rule rules out. */
101	export function WorldPicker() {

121	          <span className="ct-world-line" data-on={world.power}>{world.power ? <Zap size={15} aria-hidden="true"/> : <ZapOff size={15} aria-hidden="true"/>}Power {world.power ? 'on' : 'off'}</span>
122	          <span className="ct-world-verdict">{shown ? (world.allowed ? 'Allowed' : "Can't happen") : 'Tap to test'}</span>
123	        </button>;

151	/** A rule becomes its contrapositive in two clicks: Step 1 swaps the sides, Step 2 puts "not" on each idea.
152	 *  A "not" that was already there cancels: the idea loses its strike and turns positive. */
153	function FlipAndNegate({ left, right, captions, label, rule, result }: DemoProps) {
154	  const [stage, setStage] = useState(0);
155	  const flipped = stage >= 1;
156	  const negated = stage >= 2;
157	  const chip = (idea: DemoIdea, side: 'left' | 'right') => {
158	    const struck = !!idea.struck !== negated;
159	    return <span className="ct-chip" data-tone={idea.tone} data-side={side} data-moved={flipped} data-struck={struck}>{struck && <span className="ct-sr">not </span>}{idea.text}</span>;
160	  };
161	  return <div className="ct-flip" data-stage={stage} role="group" aria-label={label}>
162	    <p className="ct-flip-sentence" data-kind="rule"><span>The rule</span>{rule}</p>
163	    <div className="ct-flip-row">
164	      {chip(left, 'left')}
165	      <span className="ct-flip-arrow" aria-hidden="true">→</span>
166	      {chip(right, 'right')}
167	    </div>
168	    <p className="ct-flip-sentence" data-kind="result" data-show={negated} aria-hidden={!negated}><span>The contrapositive</span>{result}</p>
169	    <p className="ct-flip-caption" role="status">{captions[stage]}</p>
170	    <div className="ct-flip-controls">
171	      <button type="button" className="ct-step" data-ready={stage === 0} data-done={stage >= 1} disabled={stage !== 0} onClick={() => setStage(1)}><b aria-hidden="true">{stage >= 1 ? <Check size={16} strokeWidth={3}/> : 1}</b>Step 1. Click here to flip the sides</button>
172	      <button type="button" className="ct-step" data-ready={stage === 1} data-done={stage >= 2} disabled={stage !== 1} onClick={() => setStage(2)}><b aria-hidden="true">{stage >= 2 ? <Check size={16} strokeWidth={3}/> : 2}</b>Step 2. Click here to put “not” on both</button>
173	      {stage > 0 && <button type="button" className="ct-flip-reset" onClick={() => setStage(0)}><RotateCcw size={15} aria-hidden="true"/>Start over</button>}
174	    </div>
175	  </div>;
176	}

263	type Choice = { kind: 'reverse' | 'inverse' | 'contrapositive'; left: Idea; right: Idea; leftTone: Tone; rightTone: Tone };
264	
265	const spoken = (idea: Idea) => (idea.negated ? 'not ' : '') + idea.short.toLowerCase();

267	/** The three look-alikes for a rule, with the contrapositive at the position the case asks for. Colour follows the idea. */
268	function choicesFor(rule: RuleCase): Choice[] {
269	  const flip = (idea: Idea): Idea => ({ ...idea, negated: !idea.negated });
270	  const options: Choice[] = [
271	    { kind: 'reverse', left: rule.right, right: rule.left, leftTone: 'teal', rightTone: 'amber' },
272	    { kind: 'inverse', left: flip(rule.left), right: flip(rule.right), leftTone: 'amber', rightTone: 'teal' },
273	  ];
274	  options.splice(rule.place, 0, { kind: 'contrapositive', left: flip(rule.right), right: flip(rule.left), leftTone: 'teal', rightTone: 'amber' });
275	  return options;
276	}

278	/** Four rules, one at a time: which of three diagrams is the contrapositive? */
279	export function RuleDrill() {
280	  const [step, setStep] = useState(0);
281	  const [wrong, setWrong] = useState<number[]>([]);
282	  const [solved, setSolved] = useState<number | null>(null);
283	  const rule = ruleCases[step];
284	  const choices = choicesFor(rule);
285	  const last = step === ruleCases.length - 1;
286	  const lastTried = wrong.length ? choices[wrong[wrong.length - 1]] : null;
287	  const message = solved !== null
288	    ? rule.why.contrapositive
289	    : lastTried ? `Not that one. ${rule.why[lastTried.kind as 'reverse' | 'inverse']}` : 'Pick the diagram that says the same thing as the rule.';
290	  const next = () => { setStep(value => value + 1); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };
291	  const restart = () => { setStep(0); setWrong([]); setSolved(null); };
292	  return <div className="ct-drill" data-step={step}>
293	    <div className="ct-drill-top">
294	      <p className="ct-picker-kicker">Your turn</p>
295	      <p className="ct-drill-count">Rule {step + 1} of {ruleCases.length}<span className="ct-drill-dots" aria-hidden="true">{ruleCases.map((item, index) => <i key={item.tag} data-state={index < step ? 'done' : index === step ? 'now' : 'todo'}/>)}</span></p>
296	    </div>
297	    <p className="ct-drill-sentence">{rule.sentence}</p>
298	    <div className="ct-drill-original" role="img" aria-label={`The rule: ${spoken(rule.left)} implies ${spoken(rule.right)}`}>
299	      <Pill text={rule.left.short} tone="amber" negated={rule.left.negated}/><span className="ct-drill-arrow" aria-hidden="true">→</span><Pill text={rule.right.short} tone="teal" negated={rule.right.negated}/>
300	    </div>
301	    <p className="ct-picker-question" id={`ct-drill-q-${step}`}>Which one is the contrapositive?</p>
302	    <div className="ct-drill-options" role="group" aria-labelledby={`ct-drill-q-${step}`}>
303	      {choices.map((choice, index) => {
304	        const state = solved === index ? 'right' : wrong.includes(index) ? 'wrong' : 'open';
305	        return <button key={choice.kind} type="button" className="ct-drill-option" data-state={state} disabled={solved !== null && solved !== index} aria-label={`${spoken(choice.left)} → ${spoken(choice.right)}`} onClick={() => { if (solved !== null) return; if (choice.kind === 'contrapositive') setSolved(index); else setWrong(items => items.includes(index) ? items : [...items, index]); }}>
306	          <span aria-hidden="true" className="ct-drill-diagram"><Pill text={choice.left.short} tone={choice.leftTone} negated={choice.left.negated}/><span className="ct-drill-arrow">→</span><Pill text={choice.right.short} tone={choice.rightTone} negated={choice.right.negated}/></span>
307	          {state === 'right' && <span className="ct-drill-mark" data-kind="right"><Check size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/>Yes</span>}
308	          {state === 'wrong' && <span className="ct-drill-mark" data-kind="wrong"><X size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden="true"/>No</span>}
309	        </button>;
310	      })}
311	    </div>
312	    <p className="ct-picker-message" role="status" data-solved={solved !== null}>{message}</p>
313	    {solved !== null && (last
314	      ? <div className="ct-drill-end"><strong>That is all four. Same two steps every time.</strong><button type="button" className="ct-drill-next" onClick={restart}><RotateCcw size={15} aria-hidden="true"/>Try them again</button></div>
315	      : <button type="button" className="ct-drill-next" onClick={next}>Next rule</button>)}
316	  </div>;
317	}
318	
319	const LECTURE_TRANSCRIPT = "Here's a rule: if the ceiling light is on, then the power must be on. Now flip the question. What if the power goes out? Could the light still be on? Nope. No power, no light. That's a second rule, and it was hiding inside the first one. If the power is off, then the light must be off. That's the contrapositive. If the necessary condition is absent, the sufficient condition is absent too. Here's how you write it. Start with the rule: Light, arrow, Power. Step one: flip the sides. Power goes on the left, and Light goes on the right. Step two: put a not on both. Not power, arrow, not light. Read it back: if there's no power, the light is not on. The original and the contrapositive are logically equivalent. If one is true, the other is true, so you can use either one. They're interchangeable! Be careful, though. Only flipping the sides is not the contrapositive, and neither is only adding a not. Each one is a different rule, and the original doesn't give it to you. You need both changes together. So when you see a rule, flip it, put a not on both sides, and you've got the contrapositive. Now try it on the next few rules yourself.";
320	
321	/** Short narrated lecture. The written lesson below stands on its own if the video cannot load. */
322	export function ContrapositiveLecture() {
323	  const [failed, setFailed] = useState(false);
324	  const [read, setRead] = useState(false);
325	  return <figure className="ct-lecture">
326	    <video controls preload="metadata" playsInline poster="/media/conditionals/contrapositive/lecture.jpg" aria-label="A short lecture on the contrapositive" onError={() => setFailed(true)}>
327	      <source src="/media/conditionals/contrapositive/lecture.mp4" type="video/mp4" onError={() => setFailed(true)}/>
328	      <track kind="captions" src="/media/conditionals/contrapositive/lecture.vtt" srcLang="en" label="English"/>
329	      Your browser cannot play this video. The lesson is written out below.
330	    </video>
331	    {failed && <p role="alert" className="ct-lecture-error">The video couldn't load. The lesson is written out below.</p>}
332	    <details open={failed || undefined} onToggle={event => setRead(event.currentTarget.open)}><summary>Read the video</summary>{(read || failed) && <p>{LECTURE_TRANSCRIPT}</p>}</details>
333	  </figure>;
334	}
335	
336	/** "Pop Quiz", centred. Each letter springs up in turn once the title scrolls into view, then a band of light keeps sweeping across the lettering. */
337	export function PopQuizTitle() {
338	  const [ref, inView] = useInView<HTMLHeadingElement>();
339	  const letters = 'Pop Quiz'.split('');
340	  return <h2 ref={ref} className="ct-popquiz" data-in-view={inView} aria-label="Pop Quiz">
341	    {letters.map((letter, index) => letter === ' '
342	      ? <span key={index} className="ct-popquiz-gap" aria-hidden="true"/>
343	      : <span key={index} className="ct-popquiz-letter" aria-hidden="true" style={{ animationDelay: `${index * 0.08}s` }}>{letter}</span>)}
344	  </h2>;
345	}

import { existsSync } from 'node:fs';
import { spawn } from 'node:child_process';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { resolve } from 'node:path';

const root = fileURLToPath(new URL('../../', import.meta.url));

let activeChild;
let stopping = false;

function runNode(entry, args) {
  return new Promise((resolveExit, reject) => {
    activeChild = spawn(process.execPath, [resolve(root, entry), ...args], {
      cwd: root,
      stdio: 'inherit',
      shell: false,
    });
    activeChild.once('error', reject);
    activeChild.once('exit', (code, signal) => {
      activeChild = undefined;
      resolveExit(stopping ? 130 : code ?? (signal ? 1 : 0));
    });
  });
}

for (const signal of ['SIGINT', 'SIGTERM']) {
  process.on(signal, () => {
    stopping = true;
    if (activeChild) activeChild.kill(signal);
    else process.exitCode = 130;
  });
}

.fs-chart{width:100%;margin:1.6rem 0 .4rem;border-collapse:separate;border-spacing:0 .7rem;font-size:1.05rem;line-height:1.5}

.fs-chart-rule span{display:block;margin-bottom:.25rem;font-family:Arial,sans-serif;font-size:.72rem;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:.16em;text-transform:uppercase;color:#9fe3ea}
.fs-chart thead th{padding:.2rem 1.1rem;text-align:left;font-family:Arial,sans-serif;font-size:.74rem;font-weight:800;letter-spacing:.13em;text-transform:uppercase;color:#5d6b68}

Click Step 1.', 'Being a student is what the rule requires. Now click Step 2.', 'The requirement goes after “then.” What requires it goes after “if.”'],\n};\n\nconst fuel: FindDemo = {\n  label: 'A rule that names the requirement first',\n  parts: [{ text: 'Fuel', role: 'necessary' }, ' is necessary for ', { text: 'the car to start', role: 'sufficient' }, '.'],\n  ifIdea: 'The car starts',\n  thenIdea: 'It has fuel',\n  result: 'If the car starts, then it has fuel.',\n  captions: ['This rule names the requirement first. Click Step 1.', 'Fuel is the requirement, even though it comes first. Now click Step 2.', 'Fuel still goes after “then.” Its place in the sentence doesn’t decide its role.'],\n};\n\n/** Step 1 sweeps a highlight across the requirement in the sentence. Step 2 drops both ideas into an if-then frame. */\nfunction FindTheRequirement({ label, parts, ifIdea, thenIdea, result, captions }: FindDemo) {\n  const [stage, setStage] = useState(0);\n  return <div className=\"ns-find\" data-stage={stage} role=\"group\" aria-label={label}>\n    <p className=\"ns-find-rule\"><span>The rule</span>{parts.map((part, index) => typeof part === 'string' ? part : <mark key={index} className=\"ns-mark\" data-role={part.role} data-lit={part.role === 'necessary' ? stage >= 1 : stage >= 2}>{part.text}</mark>)}</p>\n    <div className=\"ns-frame\" data-show={stage >= 2} aria-hidden={stage < 2}>\n      <span className=\"ns-frame-word\">If</span><span className=\"ns-slot\" data-role=\"sufficient\">{ifIdea}</span>\n      <span className=\"ns-frame-word\">then</span><span className=\"ns-slot\" data-role=\"necessary\">{thenIdea}</span>\n    </div>\n    <p className=\"ns-find-result\" data-show={stage >= 2} aria-hidden={stage < 2}><span>Read it as</span>{result}</p>\n    <p className=\"ns-find-caption\" role=\"status\">{captions[stage]}</p>\n    <div className=\"ct-flip-controls\">\n      <button type=\"button\" className=\"ct-step\" data-ready={stage === 0} data-done={stage >= 1} disabled={stage !== 0} onClick={() => setStage(1)}><b aria-hidden=\"true\">{stage >= 1 ? <Check size={16} strokeWidth={3}/> : 1}</b>Step 1. Click here to find the requirement</button>\n      <button type=\"button\" className=\"ct-step\" data-ready={stage === 1} data-done={stage >= 2} disabled={stage !== 1} onClick={() => setStage(2)}><b aria-hidden=\"true\">{stage >= 2 ? <Check size={16} strokeWidth={3}/> : 2}</b>Step 2. Click here to put it after “then”</button>\n      {stage > 0 && <button type=\"button\" className=\"ct-flip-reset\" onClick={() => setStage(0)}><RotateCcw size={15} aria-hidden=\"true\"/>Start over</button>}\n    </div>\n  </div>;\n}\n\nconst groupTwoWords = ['only', 'only if', 'only when', 'only where', 'always', 'requires', 'must'];\n\n/** The one look-alike that fails beside the reading that holds. */\nfunction RequiredNotEnough() {\n  const cards: { verdict: 'wrong' | 'right'; how: string; left: string; leftTone: 'amber' | 'teal'; right: string; rightTone: 'amber' | 'teal' }[] = [\n    { verdict: 'wrong', how: 'Treat the requirement as enough.', left: 'Student', leftTone: 'teal', right: 'Enrolled', rightTone: 'amber' },\n    { verdict: 'right', how: 'Enrollment guarantees the requirement.', left: 'Enrolled', leftTone: 'amber', right: 'Student', rightTone: 'teal' },\n  ];\n  return <div className=\"ns-cards\">{cards.map(card => <article key={card.verdict} className=\"ct-move\" data-verdict={card.verdict}>\n    <p className=\"ct-move-badge\">{card.verdict === 'right' ? <Check size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden=\"true\"/> : <X size={16} strokeWidth={3} aria-hidden=\"true\"/>}{card.verdict === 'right' ? 'Right' : 'Wrong'}</p>\n    <p className=\"ct-move-how\">{card.how}</p>\n    <p className=\"ct-move-diagram\" aria-label={`${card.left} → ${card.right}`}><Pill text={card.left} tone={card.leftTone} negated={false}/><span aria-hidden=\"true\">→</span><Pill text={card.right} tone={card.rightTone} negated={false}/></p>\n  </article>)}</div>;\n}\n\nconst quiz: QuizItem[] = [\n  {\n    rule: 'Every bike in the race has working brakes.',\n    question: 'Which idea is required?',\n    options: [\n      { text: 'In the race', tone: 'amber', reason: 'Being in the race guarantees the brakes. It isn’t the requirement.' },\n      { text: 'Working brakes', tone: 'teal' },\n      { text: 'Any bike', tone: 'amber', reason: 'The rule only covers bikes in the race.' },\n    ],\n    correct: 1,\n    answer: 'If a bike is in the race, then it has working brakes.',\n  },\n  {\n    rule: 'Every bike in the race has working brakes.',\n    question: 'Kai’s bike has working brakes. Is it in the race?',\n    options: [\n      { text: 'Yes, it’s in the race.', reason: 'Brakes are required, not enough. Plenty of bikes with good brakes never race.' },\n      { text: 'No, it isn’t.', reason: 'The rule doesn’t rule the race out either.' },\n      { text: 'Nothing settles it. It may or may not be in the race.' },\n    ],\n    correct: 2,\n    answer: 'If a bike has working brakes, the rule doesn’t say whether it’s in the race.',\n  },\n  {\n    rule: 'You must show a passport to board the flight.',\n    question: 'Which idea is required?',\n    options: [\n      { text: 'Board the flight', tone: 'amber', reason: 'Boarding is what needs the passport.' },\n      { text: 'Show a passport', tone: 'teal' },\n    ],\n    correct: 1,\n    answer: 'If you board the flight, then you showed a passport.',\n  },\n  {\n    rule: 'Flour is necessary to bake this bread.',\n    question: 'Which idea goes after “then”?',\n    options: [\n      { text: 'Bake this bread', tone: 'amber', reason: 'Baking is what needs the flour, so it goes after “if.”' },\n      { text: 'Flour', tone: 'teal' },\n    ],\n    correct: 1,\n    answer: 'If you bake this bread, then you have flour.',\n  },\n];\n\nfunction Section({ title, children }: { title: string; children: ReactNode }) {\n  return <section className=\"co-section\"><div className=\"co-section-heading\"><h2>{title}</h2></div>{children}</section>;\n}\n\n/** The Necessary Condition page, built to the Contrapositive page's shape. */\nexport function NecessaryNote() {\n  return <>\n    <Section title=\"What a necessary condition is\">\n      <p className=\"ct-definition\"><strong>A necessary condition is a requirement. It has to be met, but meeting it might not be enough.</strong></p>\n      <p>You have to be a student to be enrolled in law school. Being a student is <strong>necessary</strong> for law-school enrollment. But being a student doesn’t prove you’re enrolled. You could be studying history.</p>\n    </Section>\n    <Section title=\"Where the certainty is\">\n      <p><strong>The necessary condition is what you can guarantee will occur if the sufficient condition happens.</strong></p>\n      <p>Look at the name. It's called necessary because it is necessary. If someone is enrolled in law school, being a student has to be true.</p>\n      <p>The sufficient condition opens the door. Once it's open, the necessary condition is guaranteed to be on the other side. That makes it <PulseWord>the place of certainty.</PulseWord> It's what you can prove.</p>\n    </Section>\n    <Section title=\"Watch the lesson\">\n      <SufficientConditionFilm assets={necessaryConditionFilms} title=\"Why the requirement is not enough\" ariaLabel=\"Short explanation of necessity\"/>\n    </Section>\n    <Section title=\"How to find it\">\n      <ol className=\"ct-moves\">\n        <li><strong>Step 1: Find the requirement.</strong> Ask what has to be true if the other idea happens.</li>\n        <li><strong>Step 2: Put it after “then.”</strong> The idea that requires it goes after “if.”</li>\n      </ol>\n      <FindTheRequirement {...lawSchool}/>\n      <p>Same two steps when the rule names the requirement first:</p>\n      <FindTheRequirement {...fuel}/>\n    </Section>\n    <Section title=\"Words that mark the requirement\">\n      <p>In the fuel rule, <strong>necessary</strong> marked the requirement. These Group 2 words and phrases mark it too:</p>\n      <ul className=\"ns-words\">{groupTwoWords.map(word => <li key={word}>{word}</li>)}</ul>\n      <p>The indicator is the word. The necessary condition is the whole idea it points to.</p>\n    </Section>\n    <Section title=\"What it is not\">\n      <p>A requirement is not a guarantee. Wrong answer choices count on you forgetting that.</p>\n      <RequiredNotEnough/>\n    </Section>\n    <Section title=\"Picture it on its own\">\n      <p>When you find the requirement, picture it true while the other idea is false. A history student. A car with fuel and a dead battery. If that picture fits the rule, you have a requirement, and it proves nothing by itself.</p>\n    </Section>\n    <section className=\"co-section\"><div className=\"co-section-heading\"><PopQuizTitle/></div><QuickQuiz items={quiz} idPrefix=\"nq\"/></section>\n  </>;\n}\n"}
