it('shows a rule that already has a not, and its double negative turning positive', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="contrapositive"/>);
  const demo = document.querySelectorAll('.ct-flip')[1] as HTMLElement;
  expect(demo.textContent).toMatch(/Snowing/);
  expect(demo.querySelectorAll('[data-struck=true]')).toHaveLength(1);
  fireEvent.click(within(demo).getByRole('button', { name: /Step 1/ }));
  fireEvent.click(within(demo).getByRole('button', { name: /Step 2/ }));
  const chips = [...demo.querySelectorAll('.ct-chip')].map(chip => (chip as HTMLElement).dataset.struck);
  expect(chips).toEqual(['true', 'false']);
  expect(within(demo).getByRole('status').textContent).toMatch(/the two cancel and it turns positive/);
});

it('follows the contrapositive with the four-situation chart: two rows settle something, two settle nothing', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="four-situations"/>);
  const names = ['All a rule gives you', 'Don’t fall for the necessary condition', 'Four situations', 'Ask where the fact lands'];
  for (const name of names) expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name })).toBeVisible();
  const full = document.body.textContent ?? '';
  const text = full.slice(0, full.indexOf('Pop Quiz'));
  const order = names.map(name => text.indexOf(name));
  expect(order).toEqual([...order].sort((m, n) => m - n));
  const rows = within(screen.getByRole('table')).getAllByRole('row').slice(1);
  expect(rows.map(row => row.getAttribute('data-settled'))).toEqual(['true', 'true', 'false', 'false']);
  expect(rows.map(row => within(row).getByRole('rowheader').textContent)).toEqual(['Sufficient present', 'Necessary absent', 'Sufficient absent', 'Necessary present']);
  expect(rows.slice(2).every(row => row.textContent?.includes('Nothing!'))).toBe(true);
  expect(text).not.toMatch(/raffle|breathing|air is present|promise|depends on|door|[—–~]/i);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Pop Quiz' })).toBeVisible();
});

it('quizzes all four situations on a fresh rule and explains a wrong tap', () => {
  render(<OpeningCourseContent id="four-situations"/>);
  expect(screen.getAllByText('If a library book is overdue, the borrower gets a reminder text.')).toHaveLength(1);
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Nothing. Sam may or may not get a text.' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('status')).toHaveTextContent(/^Not that one\. An overdue book is the sufficient condition/);
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Sam gets a reminder text.' }));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' }));
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Her book is overdue.' }));
  expect(screen.getByRole('status')).toHaveTextContent(/hold that is ready/);
});
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Yes, it says you win.' }));
  expect(screen.getByText('Not quite.')).toBeVisible();
  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'No, it tells you what follows if you win.' }));
  rerender(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="sufficient" url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.queryByRole('button', { name: 'No, it tells you what follows if you win.' })).toBeNull();
  rerender(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="meaning" url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'No, it tells you what follows if you win.' })).toHaveAttribute('aria-pressed', 'true');
  expect(screen.getByText('Correct.')).toBeVisible();
});

it('exposes only the permitted content and flashcard families', () => {
  const steps = OPENING_COURSE_PATH.filter(step => step.unitId === 'group-1');
  const { rerender } = render(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="necessary" steps={steps} url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'This lesson is not available here.' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(document.querySelector('.opening-course-content')).toBeNull();
  rerender(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="tools" steps={steps} url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  // The mixed word deck is the only indicator set, and it is always there.
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Indicator words' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('heading', { name: /Group [12] words/ })).toBeNull();
  const both = OPENING_COURSE_PATH.filter(step => step.unitId === 'group-1' || step.unitId === 'group-2');
  rerender(<MemoryRouter><OpeningCourseScreen view="tools" steps={both} url={url}/></MemoryRouter>);
  expect(screen.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Indicator words' })).toBeVisible();
  expect(screen.queryByRole('heading', { name: 'Sufficient and necessary' })).toBeNull();
});


function CourseNavigationHarness() {
  const location = useLocation();
  const view = location.pathname.split('/').pop() || 'lessons';
  return <><OpeningCourseNavigation view={view} url={url}/><OpeningCourseScreen view={view} url={url}/></>;
}

Group 2 intro now says the necessary condition is the part after "then" in an if-then rule and these words mark that same part. **The commit.** I moved only this session's conditional files onto a fresh branch off main, `claude/conditional-four-situations-2026-10-13`, in a new worktree at `ai-os-projects/lsatu-four-situations`. The other session's files stayed where they were, so none of them is in the commit.

**The Necessary page** now follows the Contrapositive page's shape, in this order:
1. **Definition:** your approved lead sentence in the navy panel, then the law-school example and the history student.
2. **"Where the certainty is":** your definition word for word, with the careful door image.
3. **"Watch the lesson":** the existing short video on why the requirement isn't enough.
4. **"How to find it":** two steps, then two click-through demos.
   - Step 1 finds the requirement; Step 2 puts it after "then".
   - The first demo uses the law-school rule.
   - The second uses a new rule that names the requirement first, "Fuel is necessary for the car to start." That shows its place in the sentence doesn't decide its role.
5. **Group 2 words** as a short list: only, only if, only when, only where, always, requires, must.
6. **"What it is not":** a red Wrong card (Student → Enrolled) and a green Right card (Enrolled → Student).
7. **One short habit paragraph:** "Picture it on its own".
8. **Pop Quiz:** four questions on new rules (bike brakes twice, passport, flour).

I cut the tabbed person comparison, the tabbed indicator explorer, "Two roles in the same rule", "Can a condition be both" and the old check questions. Each repeated a demo or the later Necessary-condition words page. The page went from about 665 words to roughly 450.

Source element: <main>
---
Back to lessons

CONDITIONAL REASONING

Spot the condition
01
YOUR TASK
Decide what the information proves.
01

Start with rules and facts. Decide what a rule proves on its own, what a fact adds, and test a claim against a possible case.

02

Recall what sufficient and necessary mean. Read the arrow and decide which condition belongs on each side.

03

Find the indicator words and the complete conditions they introduce. Check each answer. If it isn't right, read the hint and try again.

HOW THIS DRILL WORKS

1
Rules and facts
2
Sufficient and necessary
3
Indicator words

Step 2. Decide which condition is enough and which is required.

If the store is open, its lights are on. Which condition is sufficient?

The store is open
B
The lights are on
Start practice

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

## Current AI OS execution contract

Use the host-supported time/file tools, preserve current open state and explicit scope. Chat-only continuity remains distinct from file-writing wrapup or a broad system audit.

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
